### 2026-08-17 — Identity + Session/Request Authentication mobile client integration

**Decision:** Built the mobile client side of both already-stable, already-network-wired capabilities: `apps/mobile/src/capabilities/identity/` and `apps/mobile/src/capabilities/sessionauth/` (hand-mirrored `types.ts` + a thin HTTP client `index.ts` for all 6 Identity RPCs and all 7 Session/Request Authentication RPCs, each with its own `audit.ts` client-side stub and `DATA_MANIFEST.md`, following Cryptography & Keys' established file-per-concern pattern exactly — no codegen, per the precedent already recorded 2026-08-17 "UI integration readiness verification"); a shared `apps/mobile/src/api/httpClient.ts` (base URL from `EXPO_PUBLIC_API_URL`, defaulting to `http://localhost:8080`; typed `ApiError`; centrally-settable bearer token with per-call override); an onboarding composition layer at `apps/mobile/src/features/onboarding/` (`onboarding.ts` — `createIdentityFlow`, `restoreIdentityFlow`, `resumeSession`, `removeDevice`, `signOutEverywhere`, plus best-effort in-process automatic session renewal — and `localSession.ts`, reusing Cryptography & Keys' `secureStore.ts` directly rather than reinventing local persistence); and two screens plus a minimal recovery screen, wired via a hand-built headless custom navigator (`apps/mobile/src/navigation/SimpleStackNavigator.tsx`, built on `useNavigationBuilder`/`StackRouter`/`createNavigatorFactory` — all already transitively re-exported by the existing `@react-navigation/native` dependency, confirmed by reading its own `index.js`'s `export * from '@react-navigation/core'` chain — rather than adding `@react-navigation/native-stack` + `react-native-screens` + `react-native-safe-area-context` as new dependencies for a wiring-only pass).

**Rationale:**
- **Wire contract verified live, not assumed from source** — brought up the real server (`cd services/api && go run .` against the already-running Docker Postgres/Redis/MinIO stack) and ran two independent live checks: (1) a standalone Node script (`smoke.js`, scratchpad-only, not committed) using Node's built-in `crypto.sign`/`generateKeyPairSync` to replicate the wire protocol byte-for-byte and confirm the real server's actual behavior before writing any client code; (2) after the client code was written, a real Jest integration test (`apps/mobile/src/features/onboarding/__tests__/liveSmoke.test.ts`, gated behind `ASCEND_LIVE_SMOKE=1` so it's skipped — not failed — in the normal `npm test` run with no live server present) exercising the actual `onboarding.ts`/`identity`/`sessionauth` module code end-to-end: `createIdentityFlow` → `ListDevices`/`ListActiveSessions` → `ExportIdentity`/`ExportSessions` → `restoreIdentityFlow` (recovery-phrase path, binding a genuinely new device) → `removeDevice` → `signOutEverywhere`, all passing against the real server. Kept as a committed, opt-in regression test rather than deleted, since it costs nothing in normal CI and catches real wire-contract drift a mocked test cannot.
- **Epoch discrepancy resolved by observing the running server, not either doc comment:** `services/api/internal/identity/sign.go`'s `buildDeviceBindingMessage` doc comment claims "identity.proto's response messages... expose no epoch field," while `types.go`/`service.go` clearly populate `PublicIdentity.Epoch`/`BindDeviceResponse.Epoch`/`RevokeDeviceResponse.Epoch`. Live-confirmed via both the raw-protocol script and the real client: `CreateIdentityResponse.publicIdentity.epoch` is `0`, `BindDeviceResponse.epoch` is the epoch *after* that bind, `RevokeDeviceResponse.epoch` likewise — the field is genuinely present and correct on the wire; `sign.go`'s comment is stale (superseded by the 2026-07-16 "Follow-up: epoch discoverability closed via contract amendment" entry it doesn't reference). The mobile client (`identity/index.ts`'s `buildBindDeviceMessage` doc comment, and `onboarding.ts`'s `LocalIdentityRecord.epoch` tracking) is built against the confirmed-live behavior. Not a charter or contract change — a documentation staleness finding, worth fixing in `sign.go` in a future pass but out of this engineer's scope to touch `services/api/internal/identity`.
- **Device-key persistence gap, found and NOT worked around:** Cryptography & Keys' frozen contract has no operation to reload a specific key's raw bytes back into a signable `KeyHandle` after a process restart — `keyRegistry.ts` is intentionally volatile, and the one documented exception to "private key material never leaves this module's boundary in plaintext," `ExportKeyMaterial`, is a full, explicit, user-confirmed export of every registered key at once, not a routine per-device reload primitive (repeatedly auto-passing `user_confirmation: true` to avoid asking the user would defeat the point of that gate). Consequence, documented in `onboarding.ts`'s `activeDeviceKeyHandle` comment rather than silently accepted: automatic pre-expiry session renewal (session-authentication.charter.md §6's "fresh-signature re-issuance" commitment) works correctly for as long as the app process stays alive, but cannot re-sign after a full process restart — `resumeSession()` instead reuses a still-valid, previously-persisted `sessionToken` from `SecureLocalStore` until it naturally expires. This is a real, structural gap in Cryptography & Keys' frozen contract (no raw-key-reload seam), not an Identity/Session-Auth implementation shortfall — flagged here for the Chief Architect rather than resolved by reaching into `keyRegistry.ts`'s non-exported internals from outside the crypto module's own boundary (would violate Art. 10 and the module's own stated security guarantee). A future contract addition (e.g. a narrow `LoadPrivateKey(purpose, rawBytes) -> KeyHandle` paired with a caller-managed encrypted-at-rest store) would close it; not built here, since it would be a Cryptography & Keys charter amendment, outside this engineer's mandate.
- **Custom minimal navigator, not `@react-navigation/native-stack`:** only `@react-navigation/native` (which transitively includes `@react-navigation/core` and `@react-navigation/routers`) is an installed dependency; `native-stack` additionally requires `react-native-screens` and `react-native-safe-area-context`, neither installed, and adding native modules is a real config change out of scope for a "wiring, not polish" pass. `SimpleStackNavigator.tsx` renders only the focused screen (no transition animation/gestures) but provides real route params, back navigation, and reset — sufficient for this pass's two-and-a-half screens; screens themselves only use the standard `useNavigation`/`useRoute` hooks, so swapping in `native-stack` later requires zero screen-code changes.
- **Device signing key purpose `"sign:device-binding"`:** reused verbatim from `keyPurpose.ts`'s own documented example ("a per-device signing key" → `GenerateKeyPair({ purpose: "sign:device-binding" })"`) rather than inventing a new purpose string, keeping every device key (both the first device at `CreateIdentity` and any device bound via `BindDevice`, including the recovery path) cryptographically and semantically distinct from the identity root key's reserved `"sign:identity"` purpose.

**Article(s) invoked:** Art. 5 (explainability — client-side `logAuditEvent` stubs added to `identity/`/`sessionauth/` mirroring Cryptography & Keys' convention, explicitly documented as *not* the Art. 5 audit trail of record, which already fires server-side for every mutating RPC; verified mechanically via `scripts/constitution/check-audit-events-ts.sh`), Art. 8 (privacy — `DATA_MANIFEST.md` added for both new capability directories, verified mechanically via `scripts/constitution/check-data-manifests.sh`), Art. 9 (export — `ExportIdentity`/`ExportSessions` both wired as explicit, reachable client actions on the Devices screen, matching charter §5's "lives alongside the Devices screen as an explicit action, not hidden in a settings submenu"), Art. 10 (modularity — this client never reaches into `services/api/internal/*` or `keyRegistry.ts`'s non-exported internals; the device-key-persistence gap above is routed back rather than worked around by crossing that boundary), Art. 17 (asked first, per every prior charter in this codebase: this pass increases user ownership by making two already-built, already-frozen, already-audited capabilities actually reachable from the client a real user would use, not by expanding scope).

**Made by:** capability-engineer (Identity + Session/Request Authentication, mobile client integration).

**UNRESOLVED, routed back to Chief Architect — device-removal session-cascade gap:** Session-authentication.charter.md §5 commits to "remove this device" both revoking the device's Identity binding *and* calling `RevokeSession` for every session currently active for that `device_id`. This is not achievable with the current RPC surface: `ListActiveSessions`' `SessionSummary` carries no `session_token` (by design — see charter §3), so a client can never learn the token needed to call per-session `RevokeSession` for a device it isn't currently using; `RevokeAllSessions` is scoped to *all* of the caller's own sessions across every device, not one target device; and `ValidateSession` never re-checks device-bound status (confirmed live in `service.go`), so a revoked device's already-issued sessions survive until natural expiry regardless. Implemented for this pass as a plain `Identity.RevokeDevice` call only, with no session-revocation cascade attempt — see `apps/mobile/src/capabilities/identity/index.ts`'s `revokeDevice` doc comment and `DevicesScreen.tsx`'s removal confirmation dialog, both citing this entry explicitly. No workaround was invented (in particular, `RevokeAllSessions` was deliberately NOT silently substituted — it is over-broad and would change the promised per-device UX without authorization). **Needs a decision:** amend the Session/Request Authentication contract to add a per-device-scoped bulk revoke (e.g. `RevokeSessionsForDevice(caller_session_token, device_id)`), or accept the lesser interim guarantee ("binding removed, active sessions expire naturally") as the documented, permanent behavior.

### 2026-08-17 — Session/Request Authentication contract amendment: RevokeSessionsForDevice

**Decision:** Founder chose to amend the contract rather than accept the interim gap (previous entry). Added `RevokeSessionsForDevice(caller_session_token, device_id) -> sessions_revoked` to `docs/capabilities/session-authentication.charter.md` §3, authorized identically to `RevokeAllSessions`/`ListActiveSessions` (resolves acting identity from `caller_session_token`, never a bare `identity_ref`), scoped to sessions matching both that identity and the given `device_id`. §4 (Art. 5 audit obligation) and §5 (Devices/Sessions composition — "remove this device" now genuinely calls this RPC, not an unfulfilled promise) both updated to match. Also corrected a stale §8 line claiming this capability wasn't wired into `main.go` (it was, 2026-08-16).

**Rationale:** Founder's call — makes the charter's original §5 "remove this device" UX promise actually true instead of documenting a permanent shortfall. Routed through the standard amendment path (charter edit → guardian gate on the specific amendment → implementation) rather than resolved unilaterally by the mobile capability-engineer, per hub-and-spoke.

**Article(s) invoked:** Art. 5 (explainability), Art. 7 (a revoked device's sessions should become actually unusable, not merely eventually-expiring), Art. 12 (the single-action "remove device" UX this closes the gap for).

**Made by:** Chief Architect (charter amendment), Founder (the accept-vs-amend decision itself).

**Guardian gate:** both PASS (see charter §8 for the full table). Constitution Warden passed clean on first review (Art. 17 yes — closes a real, previously-documented gap; authorization/data-exposure/non-redundancy independently traced against the real `service.go`/`types.go`/`postgres_session_store.go`, not taken on the charter's word), with one non-blocking doc-consistency fix applied (`RevokeAllSessions`'s §3 line was missing its already-implemented `-> sessions_revoked` return annotation). Security Steward initially **blocked**: the charter left two atomicity gaps ambiguous at the same rigor this capability was already held to in its original 2026-07-17 gate — (a) a session from a concurrently in-flight `IssueSession` for the target device could survive a `RevokeSessionsForDevice` call that already returned success; (b) §5's two-call "remove device" composition (`RevokeDevice` + `RevokeSessionsForDevice`) had no stated ordering, leaving a session issued in the network gap between the two calls uncaught by either. Resolved by adding an explicit atomicity requirement to §3 (per-device serialization point; `sessions_revoked` is a completion guarantee, not a snapshot count) and an explicit ordering requirement to §5 (`RevokeDevice` called first, blocking new sessions before the revoke query runs; the composed action isn't complete until both calls succeed) — Security Steward's own required-changes text was applied verbatim, not paraphrased. Implementation must verify the serialization logic via mutation-testing, mirroring how `IssueSession`'s nonce-consumption atomicity was verified at its own merge gate.

**Next:** implementation — backend `RevokeSessionsForDevice` RPC (`services/api/internal/sessionauth`) satisfying the charter's new atomicity requirement, plus wiring the mobile Devices screen's "remove device" action to call it per the new composition ordering.

---

### 2026-08-17 — RevokeSessionsForDevice implementation, and a test-design correction

**Decision:** Implemented the gated `RevokeSessionsForDevice` RPC end to end. Backend (`services/api/internal/sessionauth`): a durable per-`(identity_ref, device_id)` generation counter (new table `sessionauth_device_generations`, migration `0007`), mirroring Identity's own `epoch` mechanism (`sign.go`) in spirit — `IssueSession` captures the device's current generation before its own verification steps and re-checks it atomically at commit time (`SessionStore.putIfDeviceGenerationUnchanged`); `RevokeSessionsForDevice` atomically bumps the generation and deletes every session for that device (`SessionStore.revokeAllForDevice`). `InMemorySessionStore` closes the race via its existing single mutex; `PostgresSessionStore` via a transaction holding a `SELECT ... FOR UPDATE` row lock on the device's generation row, bounded by a 5-second timeout (`deviceGenerationLockTimeout`) — the same bounded-lock pattern Storage's `lockBlob` was required to adopt after its own earlier Security Steward block (`docs/DECISION_LOG.md`, "Storage: lockBlob bounded-timeout fix"), applied here from the start rather than found later. Route (`POST /v1/sessionauth/sessions/revoke-device`) was already mounted via the capability's existing single `Mount(r, svc)` call in `main.go` — no composition-root change needed. Mobile: `apps/mobile/src/capabilities/sessionauth/` gained the RPC's types + client function; `onboarding.ts`'s `removeDevice` now calls `identity.revokeDevice` then `sessionauth.revokeSessionsForDevice`, per the charter's §5 ordering requirement, surfacing a partial-failure error rather than swallowing one; `DevicesScreen.tsx`'s confirmation dialog and `identity/index.ts`'s doc comment, both of which cited the now-closed gap, were updated to reflect the fix.

**A genuine bug found in the in-progress implementation, not introduced by it:** the engineer building this (mid-task, before the finding below) had already written two regression tests for the atomicity requirement, but both were wrong in ways worth recording so the pattern isn't repeated. (1) `TestRevokeSessionsForDevice_AtomicAgainstConcurrentIssueSession` fired two bare goroutines (`go func(){...}()` + `wg.Wait()`) around two trivial, non-blocking in-memory operations and asserted zero sessions must survive in *every* trial — this is flaky by construction: Go's scheduler very often runs one goroutine to completion before the other is even scheduled, and when the revoke fully completes before `IssueSession` starts, `IssueSession` legitimately captures the already-bumped generation and issues a genuinely fresh, valid session — not a race, a new authentication event for a device that was never unbound. The test failed on trial 0 or 1 on nearly every real run — not because the atomicity mechanism was broken (it wasn't), but because the test's own invariant was wrong for that legitimate ordering. Fixed by adding a nil-in-production test hook (`testHookAfterObserveGeneration`, called once, immediately after `IssueSession` reads its generation snapshot) that lets the test force the exact interleaving the charter's atomicity requirement actually names — `IssueSession`'s read strictly before the revoke's commit, its own commit strictly after — deterministically, not by scheduler luck. (2) `TestIssueSession_RejectedIfDeviceSessionsWereRevokedAfterNonceIssuedButBeforeCommit` asserted that a revoke completing entirely *before* a fresh `GetSessionChallenge`+`IssueSession` attempt even began must still cause rejection — this is stricter than the charter's actual text ("no session... can remain live... including one from a **concurrently in-flight** `IssueSession`") requires, and wrong: `RevokeSessionsForDevice` only clears currently-live sessions, it was never meant to block *future* ones — that is `Identity.RevokeDevice`'s job (checked via `DeviceResolver` at `IssueSession`'s step 2), and the real composed "remove device" flow already calls `RevokeDevice` first for exactly this reason. Replaced with `TestIssueSession_SucceedsWithFreshChallengeIssuedAfterAnEarlierUnrelatedRevoke`, asserting the correct (opposite) outcome. Both corrected tests were verified against the real, working implementation (pass, 5/5 repeated runs each) and, for the concurrent test specifically, against a deliberately reintroduced bug (`putIfDeviceGenerationUnchanged` reverted to a blind `put`, no generation check) — the corrected test caught it 3/3 runs, then the mutation was reverted and the real code re-verified passing. This is exactly the mutation-testing rigor the charter's atomicity requirement demanded, applied to the corrected test, not skipped because the original tests already existed.

**Verification:** `go build ./...` and `go test ./...` clean across every `services/api` package (not just `sessionauth`); `apps/mobile`'s `npm run typecheck` and `npm test` (49 passed, 1 opt-in skip) both clean; `scripts/constitution/run-all.sh` all 6 checks pass; live-verified the route is actually network-reachable against the real running binary (`POST /v1/sessionauth/sessions/revoke-device` with a bogus token returns `401`, not `404`).

**Article(s) invoked:** Art. 5 (explainability — `device_sessions_revoked` audit event, both server- and client-side), Art. 7 (the fix itself — a revoked device's sessions are now actually, atomically unusable, not merely eventually-expiring), Art. 10 (the generation-counter mechanism stays entirely inside `sessionauth`'s own store layer, no reach into Identity's or another capability's internals).

**Made by:** Chief Architect (diagnosed and fixed the test-design bug and completed the unfinished backend/mobile wiring after the originating capability-engineer's session stalled mid-task; the backend atomicity mechanism, migration, and Postgres row-lock implementation it had already written were sound and are unchanged).

---

### 2026-08-18 — RevokeSessionsForDevice implementation gate: independent PASS, and a retry-recoverability fix

**Decision:** Security Steward independently re-verified the implementation above (a first attempt was cut off mid-review by a platform session-limit error the day before, not by any finding — this is that review actually completing, not a second opinion after a block). It reproduced the required mutation test itself rather than trusting the prior report, and in doing so found the working tree still held live, uncommitted debris from the crashed prior capability-engineer session (`postgres_session_store.go`'s `putIfDeviceGenerationUnchanged` reverted to a blind write, a stray `.orig.bak`, and a leftover probe test) — cleaned it up, restored to HEAD, verified byte-identical before proceeding. Verdict: **PASS**, full reasoning in the charter's §8 "Implementation merge gate" table.

One real, non-blocking finding, fixed the same day: `apps/mobile`'s `removeDevice` correctly surfaces a `revokeSessionsForDevice` failure rather than swallowing it, but `Identity.RevokeDevice` is not idempotent (a second call for an already-revoked device returns `404`/`ErrDeviceNotFound`) — so a user retrying "remove device" after exactly that partial failure (device unbound, sessions not yet cleared) would have the retry's own `revokeDevice` call fail immediately, never reaching `revokeSessionsForDevice` again, leaving a bounded (≤30 min, non-renewable — the device can't get a new session, only its already-issued one lingers) residual session with no in-flow remedy. Fixed: `removeDevice` now catches a `404` from `revokeDevice` specifically, treats it as an already-satisfied precondition (not a failure), re-fetches the current epoch via the public `resolveIdentity` lookup for response-shape consistency, and proceeds to `revokeSessionsForDevice` regardless — making the composed action genuinely retry-safe.

**Rationale:** Security Steward's own standard, applied to itself: don't take a report on faith when reproducing the check directly is cheap and catches real drift (here, literal uncommitted mutation debris from a crashed session that could otherwise have been mistaken for the shipped state). The retry-recoverability gap is a real Art. 7 edge case (a user's ownership-restoring action should be safely repeatable, not silently one-shot) worth closing immediately rather than deferring, since the fix was small and well-scoped.

**Article(s) invoked:** Art. 7 (a repeated user action to cut off access should be safe to retry, not trap the user in a partially-applied state), Art. 5 (the guardian gate itself — verify, don't assert).

**Made by:** Security Steward (independent implementation gate), Chief Architect (the retry-recoverability fix, verified: `apps/mobile` typecheck/test clean, all 6 constitution checks pass).

---

### 2026-08-18 — Fixed `apps/mobile`'s entry point: package.json's `main` pointed at a file that never existed

**Decision:** Changed `apps/mobile/package.json`'s `"main"` field from `"index.ts"` (no such file ever existed in this project) to `"expo/AppEntry"` (the standard Expo entry-point convention for a plain `App.tsx`-rooted app, not using Expo Router), which resolves and registers `App.tsx`'s default export.

**Rationale:** Surfaced when the founder actually ran `expo start` for the first time — a real, pre-existing bug that every prior verification pass in this project's history had missed, because none of them actually launched Expo: `npm run typecheck` and `npm test` (the two checks every prior commit verified against) never touch `package.json`'s `main` field at all, since Jest and `tsc` don't use it. Confirmed the fix actually works, not just plausible, by bundling for real: `npx expo export --platform android` successfully resolved `expo/AppEntry.js` → `App.tsx` → `AppNavigator` and bundled all 685 modules (crypto, identity, sessionauth, navigation, screens included) with no errors — the first time this app has been proven to actually launch, as opposed to merely typecheck and unit-test clean.

**Article(s) invoked:** Art. 12 (the app should just work when launched — "simple by default" applies to development ergonomics too, not only end-user UX).

**Made by:** Chief Architect.

---

### 2026-08-18 — Superseded: `"main": "expo/AppEntry"` broke on Windows; replaced with a local `index.ts`

**Decision:** The prior fix (this same file, previous entry) resolved the missing-`main` crash but introduced a second, Windows-specific bug: resolving the bare specifier `"expo/AppEntry"` for the manifest's `mainModuleName` field produced a **backslash-separated** path (`node_modules\expo\AppEntry`), URL-encoded into the bundle request as `node_modules%5Cexpo%5CAppEntry.bundle` — Expo Go's own manifest/update-loading validation rejects this as unresolvable (the exact same `ConfigError: Cannot resolve entry file` message as the original bug, from a different cause), confirmed live via `adb logcat` against a real Android emulator (`dev.expo.updates`/`LoaderTask` logging `Failed to download remote update... {"error":"ConfigError: Cannot resolve entry file..."}`) — not assumed from the CLI's own success on the developer's non-Windows-path-sensitive `expo export` check, which had passed and masked this.

Fixed by creating `apps/mobile/index.ts` (a plain local file mirroring `node_modules/expo/AppEntry.js`'s own two lines: `registerRootComponent(App)` from `./App`) and pointing `main` back at it. A local, relative entry file resolves through Metro's ordinary project-root module resolution, not the bare-specifier/node_modules resolution path that produced the backslash corruption — confirmed live: a throwaway `expo start --port 8090` instance's manifest now reports `mainModuleName: "index.ts"` and `launchAsset.url` with clean forward slashes, and the bundle itself (`GET .../index.ts.bundle?...`) returns `200`.

**Rationale:** `expo/AppEntry` is a valid, common convention and works fine on macOS/Linux — this is specifically a Windows path-separator bug in how this Expo CLI version computes `mainModuleName` from a bare package specifier. A local `index.ts` (the more common convention in actual Expo project templates anyway) sidesteps it entirely rather than working around Expo CLI internals.

**Article(s) invoked:** Art. 12 (same as the superseded entry — verified against a real device/emulator this time, not just a bundler-level check, since that's what actually caught this).

**Made by:** Chief Architect.

**Supersedes:** the immediately preceding entry, "Fixed `apps/mobile`'s entry point: package.json's `main` pointed at a file that never existed" — that entry's diagnosis (the missing file) was correct and remains true; its specific fix (`expo/AppEntry`) is what's replaced here.

### 2026-08-18 — Added `apps/mobile/metro.config.js`: pin `projectRoot`, fixing a monorepo-detection asset-resolution bug

**Decision:** Added an explicit `metro.config.js` (`getDefaultConfig(__dirname)` from `expo/metro-config`, no other overrides) to `apps/mobile`.

**Rationale:** Found live, via `adb logcat` on a real Android emulator, while diagnosing a still-black app screen (post entry-point fix, previous two entries): Expo Go's LogBox error overlay failed to load its own `close.png` icon, Metro reporting `./../../node_modules/react-native/Libraries/LogBox/UI/LogBoxImages/close.png could not be found in the project root or any watch folder`. Root cause: with no `metro.config.js`, Expo's default config walks upward from `apps/mobile` looking for a workspace root, finds this repo's `.git` directory two levels up (`ascend/`), and — despite `ascend/` having no root `package.json` or `node_modules` of its own; this is a plain git repo with `apps/mobile` nested inside it, not an npm/pnpm workspace — treats it as a monorepo root, widening `watchFolders` and computing asset paths relative to the wrong base, producing the doubled `../..` and a genuine "not found." Confirmed the fix live: a throwaway `expo start --port 8091 --clear` instance's manifest, JS bundle (`index.ts.bundle`), and the specific previously-404ing LogBox asset URL all returned `200` after adding the config; killed before touching the founder's own running dev server.

This was masking, not causing, the actual crash under investigation — with LogBox itself unable to render, the real underlying error (a `ClassCastException` inside Expo Go's own bundled, unused-by-this-project `ExpoReanimatedPackage`, unrelated to any code in this repo) had no way to surface on-screen, presenting as a plain black screen with no diagnostic text visible to the founder. Fixing this is a precondition for actually seeing that error, not a fix for it — tracked as the next thing to diagnose.

**Article(s) invoked:** Art. 12 (a broken error-reporting path is worse than the error itself — the founder was looking at a black screen with no way to know why).

**Made by:** Chief Architect.

### 2026-08-18 — Android emulator end-to-end confirmed working; two environment findings along the way

**Decision/finding:** With the entry-point and `metro.config.js` fixes above in place, live-verified the full mobile app actually running on a real Android emulator (screenshots + `adb logcat`, not just a bundler-level check): Expo Go loads the project, the Create Identity screen renders exactly per charter §5's design, and Cryptography & Keys' on-device key generation genuinely executes (`identity_key_generated`/`key_pair_generated` audit events fired for real, correct `purpose: "sign:device-binding"`/`keyType: "ed25519"`).

Two unrelated environment issues surfaced and were resolved along the way, neither a defect in this repo's code:
1. **A stray, year-old, unrelated standalone app** (`com.anonymous.mobile`, installed 2025-07-04 on this specific emulator, clearly from unrelated prior tinkering on this machine) was also registered as an `exp://` link handler, causing Android's intent resolver to route Expo Go's own connection links to it instead — presenting as an unrelated blank screen and masking the real app entirely. Uninstalling it resolved the ambiguity. Also observed a genuine `ClassCastException` crash inside Expo Go's own bundled (project-unused) Reanimated module when this ambiguity was present; it did not recur once the stray app was removed — plausibly related resource/task contention, not conclusively root-caused, but not reproducible after cleanup either.
2. **`EXPO_PUBLIC_API_URL` set via PowerShell's `$env:` doesn't survive a session/terminal change**, and `expo start`'s dev server was restarted several times across this debugging session (to pick up the entry-point/metro-config fixes) — the variable silently reverted to its default (`http://localhost:8080`, meaningless from inside an emulator, which has its own separate loopback). Fixed durably: `apps/mobile/.env` (gitignored, machine-local) with `EXPO_PUBLIC_API_URL=http://10.0.2.2:8080` (the Android emulator's fixed host-loopback alias) — Expo CLI loads `.env` automatically on every start, removing the "remember to re-set the shell var" failure mode entirely. Added `apps/mobile/.env.example` (committed) documenting the three real cases (Android emulator / physical device / web) so this isn't rediscovered painfully again. Verified live: the compiled bundle's baked-in constant changed from `localhost:8080` to `10.0.2.2:8080` after adding the file, confirmed via a throwaway `expo start` instance before asking the founder to retest.

**Article(s) invoked:** Art. 12 (a config value that silently reverts across routine restarts is not simple by default — durable file-based config beats "remember to set this in your shell every time").

**Made by:** Chief Architect.

### 2026-08-18 — File Objects contract amendment: ListFileObjects

**Decision:** Founder's next-screen intent (see the "how do completed capabilities fit together" conversation, this date) is a personal file vault + identity_ref-based sharing, built entirely on already-`stable` capabilities — no Messaging/Discovery charter needed for this pass. Investigating File Objects' actual routes (not just the charter prose) found a real, blocking gap: no RPC anywhere lets a caller discover which `file_object_id`s exist for them — every other operation requires already knowing one. A "my files" screen was structurally impossible against the current contract, same category of gap as `RevokeSessionsForDevice`.

Added `ListFileObjects(owner, requesting_subject) -> [file_object]` to `docs/capabilities/file-objects.charter.md` §3, scoped deliberately narrow: self-only (`requesting_subject == owner`), mirroring Storage's existing `ExportAllBlobs` precedent exactly — no `CheckPermission`/Permissions involvement at all, since "list everything I own" is a right-to-see-your-own-stuff operation, not a shareable read. Reuses the existing `file_object` return shape unchanged (already confirmed leak-free of `blob_ref` by §6 point 8 — no new field, no new leak path). Deliberately does **not** solve "files shared to me" — that needs a reverse-index capability on Permissions' side that doesn't exist today, tracked as a new §7 open question rather than folded into this amendment, keeping this change narrowly scoped to File Objects' own interface (Art. 10). Also corrected a stale §8 line claiming File Objects wasn't wired into `main.go` (it was, 2026-08-16).

**Rationale:** Same reasoning as the `RevokeSessionsForDevice` amendment — makes a genuinely usable "my files" screen possible rather than shipping a client-side-only workaround (remembering IDs locally) that would silently break on reinstall/new device and couldn't discover files shared to the user anyway, which the founder explicitly wants working.

**Article(s) invoked:** Art. 1 (a user should be able to see everything they own, not just what their current device happens to remember), Art. 10 (scoped to avoid crossing into Permissions' own interface), Art. 17 (closes a real, structural gap — a files vault cannot exist without this).

**Made by:** Chief Architect (charter amendment), Founder (the decision to amend rather than ship a client-side workaround).

**Guardian gate:** pending — see charter §8 for the amendment-specific table.
